R15.2 still has \"\r\n89:                                   \"2 NOT_RUN work packages (W02, W12), release is BLOCKED without an owner seal, \"\r\n90:                                   \"and M4's semantic denominator is UNMEASURED\"),\r\n91:              \"round_id\": lease.get(\"round_id\", \"round-20261022-r15_2\"),\r\n92:              \"observed_at_utc\": time.strftime(\"%FT%TZ\", time.gmtime()),\r\n93:              \"predicate\": \"no READY agent item; every PARKED item has a typed reason and a named owner action; \"\r\n94:                           \"lease fresh or terminal proof present\",\r\n95:              \"queue_digest\": lease.get(\"queue_digest\"),\r\n96:              \"counts\": {s: sum(1 for i in q[\"items\"] if i[\"status\"] == s) for s in (\"DONE\", \"PARKED\", \"READY\")},\r\n97:              \"parked\": [{\"id\": i[\"id\"], \"reason\": i[\"parked_reason\"], \"owner_action\": i[\"owner_action\"],\r\n98:                         \"satisfiable_by\": i[\"satisfiable_by\"], \"principal_request_ref\": i[\"principal_request_ref\"]}\r\n99:                         for i in q[\"items\"] if i[\"status\"] == \"PARKED\"],\r\n100:              \"downstream_gates_not_claimed\": [\"release\", \"independent_acceptance\", \"M4\", \"R15.2 completion\"],\r\n101:              \"naming_note\": (\"this file was going to be called ROUND_CLOSE_PROOF. It is not a close proof: a state name that asserts more than its predicate checks is the DELIVERY_READY defect this round already found.\")}\r\n102:     json.dump(proof, open(PROOF, \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\", newline=\"\\n\"), ensure_ascii=False, indent=2)\r\n103:     open(PROOF, \"a\", encoding=\"utf-8\", newline=\"\\n\").write(\"\\n\")\r\n104:     print(json.dumps({\"verdict\": \"QUEUE_EXHAUSTED\", \"proof\": PROOF}, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2))\r\n105:     return 0\r\n106: \r\n107: \r\n108: if __name__ == \"__main__\":\r\n109:     raise SystemExit(main())\r\n--- FAR_STOP_LOOP_ROOT_CAUSE.md\r\n1: # FAR: why the SBL × HG-KSEOS round keeps stopping\r\n2: \r\n3: ## 1. Observed stops\r\n4: \r\n5: Twelve rounds ended immediately after a commit/push or after a measurement was written. In every case the work was\r\n6: verifiable, the tree was clean, and nothing external was blocking *at that moment*. The stop was not caused by a\r\n7: blocker; the blocker was found only after resuming.\r\n8: \r\n9: ## 2. Measurements that locate the cause\r\n10: \r\n11: | measurement | value | what it rules out |\r\n12: |---|---|---|\r\n13: | TT entries | 20 | — |\r\n14: | TT with a prose `next_legal_step` | 20 | the round does record intent |\r\n15: | TT with an executable `resume_cmd` | **0** | intent is never machine-runnable |\r\n16: | Denominator items | 56 (18 W + 25 C + 9 E + 4 M) | — |\r\n17: | Denominator items with `next_action` | **0** | the register measures state, it cannot drive work |\r\n18: | Kanban tasks | 18 | — |\r\n19: | Kanban `task_runs` | 6 | the board was never used as a dispatch queue |\r\n20: | Kanban cards still `blocked` with reason `initial_status` | 12 | blocked-by-default reads as \"nothing to do\" |\r\n21: \r\n22: ## 3. Root cause\r\n23: \r\n24: **The round's artefacts describe state; none of them carries an executable next action.** At a turn boundary the agent\r\n25: re-derives \"what now\" from prose, and prose does not survive context compression. So the loop terminates not because\r\n26: the work is finished but because the *only available move* is to report.\r\n27: \r\n28: This is a system defect, and it has three components:\r\n29: \r\n30: 1. **No executable queue.** Status registers, TT registers and the kanban board all answer \"where are we\"; none answers\r\n31:    \"what do I run next, with which command, and what proves it worked\".\r\n32: 2. **Blocked reads as stopped.** 12 of 18 cards were blocked with `initial_status`, a state that carries no reason and\r\n33:    no next action — so a reader cannot distinguish \"waiting on an owner\" from \"not started\".\r\n34: 3. **No retry ladder.** The first EXECUTE dispatch died at 300 s and the round reported instead of re-scoping the task.\r\n35:    The same task later succeeded in 344 s once re-scoped; the failure was survivable and was treated as terminal.\r\n36: \r\n37: ## 4. Repair, chosen for leverage\r\n38: \r\n39: **A queue whose items are commands, plus a driver that parks blockers and keeps going.**\r\n40: \r\n41: - `QUEUE_R15_2.json`: every item carries `id`, `status`, `owner_class` (`AGENT` / `OWNER` / `HOST`), `next_action`,\r\n42:   and for agent items a literal `resume_cmd` with a `expect` condition.\r\n43: - `tools/advance_queue.py`: runs ready `AGENT` items one at a time, records the observed output, marks `DONE` on the\r\n44:   expectation, and on failure **parks** the item with a typed reason and continues to the next one.\r\n45: - Stop policy, now explicit and the only legal stops: destructive action, missing credential, mandated lane\r\n46:   unavailable, or an owner-only decision. Everything else parks and the driver continues.\r\n47: \r\n48: Design note: the driver deliberately does not try to be a scheduler. It is a resume pointer with a retry ladder; the\r\n49: state it needs is a file, so it survives context compression, a new session, and a reboot.\r\n50: \r\n51: ## 5. Why this is better than \"try harder\"\r\n52: \r\n53: A resolve-to-continue instruction fails exactly when context is compressed, which is when the work is longest. A queue\r\n54: file plus a driver moves the memory from the conversation into the repository, so \"continue\" becomes a command rather\r\n55: than a recollection. It is also auditable: the queue records what ran, what it printed, and what was parked.\r\n--- FAR_AUTONOMY_ROOT_CAUSE.md\r\n1: # FAR, architecture level: continuation has no owner\r\n2: \r\n3: The previous FAR found that the round's artefacts described state and carried no executable next action. That was\r\n4: true, and fixing it produced a queue and a driver. It did not stop the round from stopping, because the queue is still\r\n5: **pulled by the conversation**. This document is about that second layer.\r\n6: \r\n7: ## 1. Evidence\r\n8: \r\n9: | observation | value | what it means |\r\n10: |---|---|---|\r\n11: | rounds that ended with work still READY | 12 | the loop's termination point was a *turn*, not a state |\r\n12: | queue created at | stop #12 | the mechanism that prevents stopping was built after eleven stops |\r\n13: | scheduled jobs that ran the driver | **0** | nothing outside the conversation could resume the round |\r\n14: | parked items with an owner action | 0 before the FAR pack | a parked item had no next actor, so parking looked like ending |\r\n15: | stop reason recorded in a governed object | **none** | no control-plane record distinguishes \"done\" from \"out of turn\" |\r\n16: \r\n17: ## 2. Root cause, stated architecturally\r\n18: \r\n19: **Continuation is owned by the conversational turn.** Three components are missing, and each one is a component, not a\r\n20: habit:\r\n21: \r\n22: 1. **No lease.** Nothing in SBL or HG-KSEOS records \"this round is still in flight, with these remaining items, until\r\n23:    this time\". Without a lease there is no fact of the matter about whether the round is running or abandoned — so a\r\n24:    turn boundary is indistinguishable from a completion.\r\n25: 2. **No close guard.** Any principal can declare a round finished. There is no predicate that must hold before a\r\n26:    closure is even expressible: no requirement that the ready set is empty, that every parked item carries a typed\r\n27:    reason and a named next actor, and that a terminal proof exists.\r\n28: 3. **No watchdog.** The driver exists but nothing invokes it except a human turn. A loop whose only scheduler is the\r\n29:    thing it is supposed to outlive is not autonomous; it is manual with good bookkeeping.\r\n30: \r\n31: The `DELIVERY_READY` finding this round is the same defect in miniature: the state name asserts readiness while the\r\n32: predicate that would justify the name is not checked. **State names without predicates are how both systems lie.**\r\n33: \r\n34: ## 3. Repair\r\n35: \r\n36: ### 3.1 Runtime side (implemented here)\r\n37: \r\n38: - `ROUND_LEASE.json` — `round_id`, `subject_sha`, `denominator_ref`, `ready_set_ref`, `driver_cmd`,\r\n39:   `stop_conditions[4]`, `heartbeat_utc`, `ttl_minutes`, `terminal_proof`. Written by the driver on every step; its\r\n40:   heartbeat is the evidence that the round is alive.\r\n41: - `tools/heartbeat.py` — stamps the lease and records the queue digest, so a stale lease is detectable.\r\n42: - `tools/continuation_guard.py` — the close predicate. A round may be declared terminal only when: no `AGENT` item is\r\n43:   READY; every PARKED item has `parked_reason` **and** `owner_action`; and the lease heartbeat is fresh or a terminal\r\n44:   proof exists. Otherwise it exits non-zero and prints the blocking ids. This is what makes \"we are done\" a claim that\r\n45:   can fail.\r\n46: - **A scheduled job** runs `advance_queue.py` + `continuation_guard.py` on an interval, so the loop advances with no\r\n47:   conversation at all. This is the piece that actually changes the outcome.\r\n48: \r\n49: ### 3.2 Control-plane side (candidate diff, same shape as W15)\r\n50: \r\n51: HG-KSEOS should own the lease, not the round tree: a `continuation_leases` table with\r\n52: `(round_id, subject_sha, denominator_ref, heartbeat_utc, ttl_minutes, terminal_proof, closed_by)` plus a\r\n53: `continuation-guard` refusal in `project advance` when a project claims a terminal state without a guard proof. A\r\n54: candidate diff and its tests are produced; applying it is an HGK owner action, exactly like the `DELIVERY_READY` guard.\r\n55: \r\n56: ### 3.3 Why not \"more resolve\"\r\n57: \r\n58: Instructions decay with context; a cron entry and a predicate do not. The test of this repair is not whether the round\r\n59: runs longer in one turn — it is whether a *new* session, with no memory of this one, can resume the round from files\r\n60: alone and be **refused** when it tries to close prematurely.\r\n--- CONTRACT.json\r\n1: {\r\n2:   \"schema\": \"CAPC-PROMPT-CONTRACT/1\",\r\n3:   \"task_id\": \"SBL-R15.2-CONTINUATION-20261022\",\r\n4:
